# Dead code candidates, 11 Sep 2026

Read-only recommendation packet. Branch `fe/css`, tree clean at the commit
recorded below before the first read (G1). This file is the only file
written; nothing was deleted or edited.

## Ruling this packet is built against

Per `docs/reviews/ROUTE-STATUS-2026-09-11.md`, Brian's ruling of 11 Sep
2026: the live route set is the 92 Current routes. The 8 Superseded routes
get no CSS session and go to Nick as a recommendation. The 5 Unknown
routes are excluded from the sweep until Nick classifies them. This packet
carries that same exclusion forward: the 5 Unknown routes are excluded
from every tier below and listed separately.

## Scope

The graph covers every `.js`/`.jsx`/`.ts`/`.tsx`/`.mjs`/`.css` file under
`app/`, `components/`, `lib/`, `data/`, and `services/`, plus every
`content/**/*.md` data file: 3,072 files total, app/api routes,
fixtures, `*.contract.js` files, diagnostics scripts, and ViewModels
included, exactly as the brief asked. Explicitly out of scope, and not
tiered below: `public/` (binary assets, not import-graph nodes in this
codebase), `ds-bundle/` and `.design-sync/` (a separate design-sync
build/tooling output, confirmed by grep to have zero import edges to or
from `app/`/`components/`/`lib/`), and `bible/`/`docs/` (prose, not
code). Two `data/*.bk` backup files (`factions.bk`, `locations.bk`) were
also excluded: their extension makes them unimportable by construction,
so they are trivially dead, not a graph finding.

## Method

A static import graph was built by direct source scan of the whole repo
(not from any consumer list in either review document), following:

- ES `import`/`export ... from`, CommonJS `require`, and dynamic
  `import()` (including `next/dynamic(() => import(...))`), resolved
  through the `@/*` -> repo root alias declared in `jsconfig.json` and
  through relative paths, with `.js`/`.jsx`/`.ts`/`.tsx`/`.mjs`/`.css`
  and `/index.*` fallback resolution.
- CSS `@import` chains.
- Next.js App Router conventions: every route's own `layout.js`,
  `template.js`, `loading.js`, `error.js`, `not-found.js`, and
  `default.js` from its directory up to `app/` (a layout wraps its
  routes without a literal import, so these are added as extra roots,
  matching the same convention `docs/reviews/CSS-SWEEP-AUDIT-2026-09-11.md`
  section 7 describes for its own import trace), plus `app/middleware.js`
  and `app/manifest.js` as global roots.
- `app/api/**/route.js` handlers: matched to the client call sites that
  invoke them via a path-pattern regex tolerant of template-literal
  interpolation and multi-line construction (a plain literal-substring
  match badly undercounted these on the first pass; the fix is recorded
  in the diff history of this analysis; the regex fell back further to a
  "final path segment chosen at runtime from a named literal elsewhere in
  the same file" check for handler-dispatch clients).
- `content/**/*.md` data files: wired to the `data/*.js` file that loads
  their collection through `lib/content.js`'s
  `getAllMarkdownEntries(collection)` / `getMarkdownEntryByPath(collection,
  slug)`, so a collection directory nothing calls shows correctly as
  orphaned (this reproduces, independently, the same finding
  `ROUTE-STATUS-2026-09-11.md` already made by hand for `content/chronicle`).

Three route sets were traced independently through this graph: the 92
Current routes, the 8 Superseded routes, and the 285 retired
`app/dev/ui-preview/**` preview routes (the count is confirmed by direct
`find`, and matches `docs/reviews/CSS-SWEEP-AUDIT-2026-09-11.md` section 1's
own count exactly). The 5 Unknown routes were never used as reachability
roots for any tier, per the ruling above.

## The critical rule, applied

A file is never called dead because a document's stated "consumers" say
so. Two independent checks ran against live source before any file
entered a delete tier:

1. Every import edge above was extracted straight from the files as they
   exist on disk right now, not copied from either review document's
   prose.
2. A second, adversarial pass then re-derived reachability a different
   way for every delete-tier candidate: a whole-word, `\b`-bounded
   basename search across every file this graph marks live (Keep + Tier
   E), independent of the parsed import edges. 1,556 candidates were
   checked this way; 56 produced a live-code hit and were manually run
   down to a specific import statement before being cleared. All 56
   resolved to one of two known false-positive shapes in this codebase,
   confirmed by reading the actual resolving import in each case (not
   assumed from the pattern):
   - a generic-English-word basename (`chronicle`, `navigation`,
     `stories`, `form`) colliding with unrelated prose or an unrelated
     identifier;
   - the repo's "LOOM" refactor pattern, where an old flat file (for
     example `components/studio/StudioCharacterCard.jsx`) and a new
     nested `<feature>/<Feature>.view.jsx` file share an identical
     basename; the live import resolves to the new nested file every
     time this was checked, leaving the old flat file dead.
3. A further independent workflow ran adversarial re-verification
   agents against a stratified sample (every branch-diff-flagged
   delete-tier file, all of Tier C, all of Tier D, and a 320-file random
   sample split across Tiers A and B) with instructions to refute the
   tentative tier by finding a real live import, not a text match. See
   the verification results below.
4. `docs/reviews/CSS-SWEEP-AUDIT-2026-09-11.md` independently traced its
   own static import graph from the same 105 routes for its CSS sweep
   (section 7) and separately flagged several of the same files as
   product orphans by hand (for example `Adventures.jsx`,
   `StoriesV2Mockup.jsx`, `ImagesV2Mockup.jsx`, the whole
   `components/studio/chat/*` flat package, `KitCreditsModal.jsx`, and
   the `image-studio/v2-concept/*` package -- section 9749, 1559, 941,
   894, 6515, 7575). Every one of those citations agrees with this
   packet's tier for the same file; they are cited inline in the tables
   below as extra corroboration where they apply.


## Verification workflow addendum

A 9-agent workflow independently re-verified a stratified sample of 401
delete-tier candidates against live source, with instructions to refute
the tentative tier by finding a real live import, not a text match. The
sample covered: all 72 delete-tier files that also appear in one of the
three branch diffs, all 13 Tier C files, all 8 Tier D files, and a
320-file random sample split across Tiers A and B. Each agent was told
to distinguish an actual resolving import from a coincidental text hit
(a comment, a JSDoc type annotation, a diagnostics file reading a path as
a string, a duplicate-basename LOOM-refactor collision).

Result: 401/401 CONFIRMED_DEAD, 0 disputed. Representative evidence
returned by the agents (a small sample of the full 401, illustrating the
kinds of near-misses they ruled out): `components/kit/dropdown/KitDropdown.contract.js`
and `components/kit/image-editor/KitImageEditor.contract.js` each had
their only hit be a JSDoc `@property {import(...)}` type-annotation
comment in a sibling `.contract.js` file, not a runtime import;
`components/kit/global-search/KitGlobalSearch.contract.js` and four
`KitImageCreatorPanel.contract.js` hits were diagnostics files reading
the path as a string, not importing it; `app/studio/v2/images/images-live/imagesV2LiveAdapterDiagnostics.mjs`'s
only hit was a code comment in `ImagesV2Live.jsx` referencing the
diagnostics file by name, not importing it. No sampled file surfaced a
genuine resolving import outside the retired preview harness or the 8
Superseded route files.

This does not certify all 1,556 delete-tier files individually (a
401-file sample was chosen for depth over exhaustive re-coverage, since
the deterministic graph plus the whole-codebase basename cross-check
already covered 100% of them); it is strong evidence the tiering method
itself is sound and did not systematically miss a reachability pattern.

## Tier A: preview harness (888 files, ~77,764 lines)

file reachable from them and from nothing else (no Current route, no
Superseded route). Per `docs/reviews/CSS-SWEEP-AUDIT-2026-09-11.md`

page files; per its memory note, it is retired and never product.
"Imported by" for a bare preview page file is empty by construction (a
page file is an entry point, nothing imports it); the harness's own

## Tier B: orphans (647 files, ~57,907 lines)

No route of any status reaches these at all -- not Current, not
Superseded, not the preview harness. Two large, well-evidenced clusters
inside this tier are worth naming up front rather than reading as 647
unrelated rows:

- **The pre-chassis-extraction `lib/server/services/creations/*` cluster**
  (`getOwnedCreationForEdit.js`, `listOwnedCreationSummaries.js`,
  `listPublicCreationSummaries.js`, `attachFeaturedImageSlotsToCreationRows.js`,
  `creationAttribution.js`, and siblings). Confirmed by direct read:
  every live `app/api/creations/**/route.js` handler now calls
  `crestfallApiRequest()` (`lib/server/api/crestfallApiClient.js`),
  which proxies to the external Crestfall backend via
  `CRESTFALL_API_INTERNAL_URL` -- not these local service functions.
  This matches the standing rule in this repo's `CLAUDE.md`
  (FE-REVIEW-01: "Chassis routes and application logic remain in
  Crestfall; Crestfall-fe is Views, Kit, tokens, fixtures, and page
  composition only"): this cluster is exactly the application logic
  that rule says should not live here, and the API routes were
  converted to thin proxies, leaving it stranded. A live, differently
  named cluster (`lib/shared/creations/creationAttribution.js`,
  `lib/server/creations/createCreation.js`, etc.) covers the same
  ground today and is correctly Keep/Tier E -- confirmed by reading the
  actual resolving import in each case, not assumed from the similar
  name.
- **Component-level `*Diagnostics.mjs` / `*Harness.mjs` / `*Audit.mjs`
  test files** (~227 files). These are never imported by any other file
  -- they are `node --test` / `node script.mjs` entry points invoked
  directly by a `package.json` script, which is not a route-reachability
  edge, so they correctly land here by this packet's tiering rule. Most
  are wired to a real `npm run diagnostics:*` script that still resolves
  to an existing file in this repo (125 files across all tiers have such
  a script reference; the "Branch flag" column does not capture this --
  check the file's basename against `package.json`'s `scripts` block
  before deleting anything in this tier that looks like a test). A
  smaller number (for example the `services/api/src/services/chat/telemetry/*`
  files, and two `mc7*Diagnostics.mjs` files under
  `components/studio/my-creations/edit/sections/mechanics-modules/mechanics-presets/`)
  import sibling files or `services/engine-middleware/` paths that do
  not exist anywhere in this repo at all -- these are broken even on
  their own terms, not just unreachable from a route.
- **Duplicate API route**: `app/api/profile/[username]/public/route.js`
  (singular) has zero references anywhere in the codebase, while
  `app/api/profiles/[username]/public/route.js` (plural) is the one
  every live client actually calls -- a likely leftover typo/duplicate.

## Tier E: needs extraction

1,070 files are reachable from at least one Current route AND at least
one Superseded-or-preview route. None of these are deleted by this
packet. They split into two very different buckets:

### Tier E, shared with a Superseded route (230 files) -- read this part

These are the files where deleting Tier C/D could actually threaten a
Current route, because a Superseded route reaches the same file a
Current route needs. Concretely, this cluster is dominated by three
patterns, each already confirmed by direct read, not inferred:

1. **Whole-app Studio shell** (`components/studio/StudioShell*`,
   `StudioSidebar*`, `StudioTopBar*`, `StudioMobileNav*`,
   `StudioAccountProvider*`, `app/studio/layout.js`, and the shared
   `components/kit/*` / `components/ui/*` primitives they render). Every
   `/studio/**` route mounts this shell, Current and Superseded alike,
   because `app/studio/layout.js` wraps the whole subtree. Deleting the
   8 Superseded route folders removes 8 leaf pages; it does not touch
   the shell, which stays exactly as reachable as it is today. No
   extraction needed.
2. **The shared Story Chat implementation**
   (`app/studio/v2/stories/[id]/StoryChatPage.jsx` and everything under
   `components/studio/story-rooms/**` it renders, plus the
   `app/api/studio/story-rooms/[id]/**` backend routes). `ROUTE-STATUS-2026-09-11.md`
   already established this by hand: the Superseded route
   `app/studio/story-rooms/[id]/page.js` is a thin wrapper whose own file
   comment reads "the V2 route owns Story Chat; both routes mount the
   exact same implementation." The code is not diverged, it is the
   identical file used by both routes. Deleting the Superseded wrapper
   page (Tier D) does not remove anything the Current route needs. No
   extraction needed, just the wrapper page goes.
3. **Character-creation modals** (`CharacterCreatorModal.jsx` and
   siblings under `components/studio/create/*/creator-stops/`, plus the
   color/hair/skin/voice picker modals they open). These are mounted by
   the Current `/studio/v2/studio` hub (embedded flow) and, transitively,
   also sit downstream of the Superseded `/studio/create/character`
   flow's own view-model. Same code, two entry points; no divergent
   behavior was found in this pass. No extraction needed.

Nick should still skim the full table below before deleting Tier C/D --
it is the accurate list of what is jointly reachable -- but the
recommendation is: delete Tier D wholesale, and nothing in this list
needs to move first.

### Tier E, shared only with the retired preview harness (840 files)

This is the much larger remainder: real, live, Current-route-owned
components and libraries (most of `components/kit/*`, most of the v2
page shells, Studio Economy Widget, the character customization modal
stack, etc.) that the retired `app/dev/ui-preview` harness ALSO renders
in isolation as a design-review fixture. That is the harness's whole
purpose -- it exists to preview real production components -- so this
overlap is expected, not a defect. Deleting Tier A (the harness itself)
does not touch a single one of these 840 files; they stay exactly as
reachable as they are today, owned entirely by their Current callers.
Listed below for completeness only; no action follows from this bucket.

## How to use this

These are recommendations for Nick to rule on, not instructions. Nothing
in this repository was deleted, moved, or edited to produce this packet
(the only file this pass wrote is this one). Tier A, B, and C are
candidates for deletion; Tier D is 8 specific route files/folders; Tier E
is never a deletion candidate. Before deleting anything in Tier A, B, C,
or D, re-run a targeted grep for the file's own basename against current
`main`/`fe/css`, since this snapshot is only as fresh as the commit
recorded at the top -- new work landing after that commit is not
reflected here.
